Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or worried about these mistakes or forgetfulness?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say anxious exactly… maybe a little self-conscious about it, if that makes sense. I don’t sit around worrying all day, but when I realize I’ve mixed something up, I do feel embarrassed and I start hoping nobody thinks I’m being careless.\n\nSo… yes, maybe some worry, but not in a big way. I’m not sure if that’s the kind of thing you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. It’s small stuff, but it’s been happening enough that I’m feeling a little foolish about it.\n\nDoctor: Are these mistakes causing you any significant problems or interfering with your daily activities?\n\nPatient: Not major problems, I don’t think… I mean, I can still get through my day. But it has affected a few things. I’ve kind of stepped back from helping lead a couple of the DIY workshops, just because I don’t want my little mix-ups to cause trouble for other people.\n\nAnd at home, my wife has started double-checking plans with me—like family visits or things at the center—so I don’t accidentally get a time or detail wrong and throw things off. It’s a bit embarrassing to say, but I suppose that counts as interfering some, doesn’t it?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're forgetting things more often than you used to?\n\nPatient: Maybe a little, yes. I wouldn’t say I’m forgetting important things all the time or anything like that, but I do spend more time than I’d like looking for things I just had in my hand—my keys, reading glasses, tape measure, that little bag I use for card games.\n\nIt’s usually just a few minutes, and I do find them, so maybe that’s normal to some extent… but it happens enough that I notice it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed these mistakes and forgetfulness getting worse over time?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure, to be honest. I don’t know that I could say it’s clearly getting worse over time. It’s more that I’ve started noticing it more, maybe because it’s embarrassing when it happens.\n\nMy wife might have a better sense of whether it’s increasing, but from my side… I’d say it comes and goes a bit. Nothing dramatic, I don’t think. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or worried about these mistakes or forgetfulness?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say anxious exactly… maybe a little self-conscious about it, if that makes sense. I don’t sit around worrying all day, but when I realize I’ve mixed something up, I do feel embarrassed and I start hoping nobody thinks I’m being careless.\n\nSo… yes, maybe some worry, but not in a big way. I’m not sure if that’s the kind of thing you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I have my moments like anyone, I suppose, especially when I feel a bit foolish about forgetting something, but I wouldn’t say I’ve been sad or down lately.\n\nI think overall my mood’s been okay. Does that sound like what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your ability to learn new things or follow instructions?\n\nPatient: Maybe a little, though I wouldn’t want to overstate it. It’s not that I can’t learn things, exactly. It’s more like… if someone tells me something out loud, I don’t always take it in the first time.\n\nLike my wife will be looking right at me and say, “Dave, the cards are in the hall closet now,” and then a minute later I realize I heard her talking, but I didn’t actually catch where she said they were. It’s a bit embarrassing. Is that the sort of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself struggling to find the right words when you're trying to express something?\n\nPatient: Not that I’ve really noticed, no. I mean, once in a while I might lose a word, but I think that happens to everybody, doesn’t it?\n\nI don’t feel like I’m regularly stuck trying to say what I mean. At least, not enough that I’d have thought to mention it. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your ability to concentrate or pay attention?\n\nPatient: A little, yes. Not terrible, I don’t think, but I do notice it sometimes.\n\nLike at the senior center, if I’m filling something out or working on one of those little projects, and people are talking at the next table, or my phone lights up, my mind just sort of jumps over there. Then I look back and I’ve lost where I was.\n\nIt’s not that I can’t concentrate at all… it’s more that I get pulled off track pretty easily. Is that helpful?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’ve noticed anything major, no. I mean, sleep can be a little up and down at my age, I suppose, but I wouldn’t say there’s been some big change lately.\n\nI still get to bed and get up… more or less normally. Nothing that jumped out at me as a real problem. It’s small stuff, but it’s been happening enough that I’m feeling a little foolish about it.\n\nDoctor: Are these mistakes causing you any significant problems or interfering with your daily activities?\n\nPatient: Not major problems, I don’t think… I mean, I can still get through my day. But it has affected a few things. I’ve kind of stepped back from helping lead a couple of the DIY workshops, just because I don’t want my little mix-ups to cause trouble for other people.\n\nAnd at home, my wife has started double-checking plans with me—like family visits or things at the center—so I don’t accidentally get a time or detail wrong and throw things off. It’s a bit embarrassing to say, but I suppose that counts as interfering some, doesn’t it?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're forgetting things more often than you used to?\n\nPatient: Maybe a little, yes. I wouldn’t say I’m forgetting important things all the time or anything like that, but I do spend more time than I’d like looking for things I just had in my hand—my keys, reading glasses, tape measure, that little bag I use for card games.\n\nIt’s usually just a few minutes, and I do find them, so maybe that’s normal to some extent… but it happens enough that I notice it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed these mistakes and forgetfulness getting worse over time?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure, to be honest. I don’t know that I could say it’s clearly getting worse over time. It’s more that I’ve started noticing it more, maybe because it’s embarrassing when it happens.\n\nMy wife might have a better sense of whether it’s increasing, but from my side… I’d say it comes and goes a bit. Nothing dramatic, I don’t think. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or worried about these mistakes or forgetfulness?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say anxious exactly… maybe a little self-conscious about it, if that makes sense. I don’t sit around worrying all day, but when I realize I’ve mixed something up, I do feel embarrassed and I start hoping nobody thinks I’m being careless.\n\nSo… yes, maybe some worry, but not in a big way. I’m not sure if that’s the kind of thing you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I have my moments like anyone, I suppose, especially when I feel a bit foolish about forgetting something, but I wouldn’t say I’ve been sad or down lately.\n\nI think overall my mood’s been okay. Does that sound like what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your ability to learn new things or follow instructions?\n\nPatient: Maybe a little, though I wouldn’t want to overstate it. It’s not that I can’t learn things, exactly. It’s more like… if someone tells me something out loud, I don’t always take it in the first time.\n\nLike my wife will be looking right at me and say, “Dave, the cards are in the hall closet now,” and then a minute later I realize I heard her talking, but I didn’t actually catch where she said they were. It’s a bit embarrassing. Is that the sort of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself struggling to find the right words when you're trying to express something?\n\nPatient: Not that I’ve really noticed, no. I mean, once in a while I might lose a word, but I think that happens to everybody, doesn’t it?\n\nI don’t feel like I’m regularly stuck trying to say what I mean. At least, not enough that I’d have thought to mention it. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your ability to concentrate or pay attention?\n\nPatient: A little, yes. Not terrible, I don’t think, but I do notice it sometimes.\n\nLike at the senior center, if I’m filling something out or working on one of those little projects, and people are talking at the next table, or my phone lights up, my mind just sort of jumps over there. Then I look back and I’ve lost where I was.\n\nIt’s not that I can’t concentrate at all… it’s more that I get pulled off track pretty easily. Is that helpful?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’ve noticed anything major, no. I mean, sleep can be a little up and down at my age, I suppose, but I wouldn’t say there’s been some big change lately.\n\nI still get to bed and get up… more or less normally. Nothing that jumped out at me as a real problem. Is there a particular kind of change you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your ability to manage everyday tasks and responsibilities has changed over the past year?\n\nPatient: I suppose… yes, a bit. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, because I’m still managing most things day to day.\n\nBut I have had a few mix-ups with plans and responsibilities. Enough that I’ve sort of stepped back from helping lead a couple of the DIY workshops at the center, just so I don’t throw things off. And my wife has been double-checking plans with me more, like for family visits or center activities.\n\nIt’s not like everything has fallen apart, but I guess there has been some change. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any difficulty remembering recent events or learning new information?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in any clear way. I mean, I might forget little things here and there, but I don’t feel like I’m forgetting recent events or unable to learn something new.\n\nIf someone explains something to me and I’m paying attention, I usually get it. So… no, I wouldn’t say that’s been a real problem. Does that sound like what you’re asking?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

I’m not sure if that’s the kind of thing you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I have my moments like anyone, I suppose, especially when I feel a bit foolish about forgetting something, but I wouldn’t say I’ve been sad or down lately.\n\nI think overall my mood’s been okay. Does that sound like what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your ability to learn new things or follow instructions?\n\nPatient: Maybe a little, though I wouldn’t want to overstate it. It’s not that I can’t learn things, exactly. It’s more like… if someone tells me something out loud, I don’t always take it in the first time.\n\nLike my wife will be looking right at me and say, “Dave, the cards are in the hall closet now,” and then a minute later I realize I heard her talking, but I didn’t actually catch where she said they were. It’s a bit embarrassing. Is that the sort of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself struggling to find the right words when you're trying to express something?\n\nPatient: Not that I’ve really noticed, no. I mean, once in a while I might lose a word, but I think that happens to everybody, doesn’t it?\n\nI don’t feel like I’m regularly stuck trying to say what I mean. At least, not enough that I’d have thought to mention it. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your ability to concentrate or pay attention?\n\nPatient: A little, yes. Not terrible, I don’t think, but I do notice it sometimes.\n\nLike at the senior center, if I’m filling something out or working on one of those little projects, and people are talking at the next table, or my phone lights up, my mind just sort of jumps over there. Then I look back and I’ve lost where I was.\n\nIt’s not that I can’t concentrate at all… it’s more that I get pulled off track pretty easily. Is that helpful?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’ve noticed anything major, no. I mean, sleep can be a little up and down at my age, I suppose, but I wouldn’t say there’s been some big change lately.\n\nI still get to bed and get up… more or less normally. Nothing that jumped out at me as a real problem. Is there a particular kind of change you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your ability to manage everyday tasks and responsibilities has changed over the past year?\n\nPatient: I suppose… yes, a bit. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, because I’m still managing most things day to day.\n\nBut I have had a few mix-ups with plans and responsibilities. Enough that I’ve sort of stepped back from helping lead a couple of the DIY workshops at the center, just so I don’t throw things off. And my wife has been double-checking plans with me more, like for family visits or center activities.\n\nIt’s not like everything has fallen apart, but I guess there has been some change. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any difficulty remembering recent events or learning new information?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in any clear way. I mean, I might forget little things here and there, but I don’t feel like I’m forgetting recent events or unable to learn something new.\n\nIf someone explains something to me and I’m paying attention, I usually get it. So… no, I wouldn’t say that’s been a real problem. Does that sound like what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your mental sharpness has decreased compared to a few years ago?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’d say it’s really decreased, no. Maybe I’m a little slower to get going with things than I used to be, but that could just be age, couldn’t it?\n\nI still feel like I can follow conversations and understand things when I’m focused. So… I wouldn’t want to say I’m less sharp exactly. Does that seem reasonable?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I mean, once in a while I might lose a word, but I think that happens to everybody, doesn’t it?\n\nI don’t feel like I’m regularly stuck trying to say what I mean. At least, not enough that I’d have thought to mention it. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your ability to concentrate or pay attention?\n\nPatient: A little, yes. Not terrible, I don’t think, but I do notice it sometimes.\n\nLike at the senior center, if I’m filling something out or working on one of those little projects, and people are talking at the next table, or my phone lights up, my mind just sort of jumps over there. Then I look back and I’ve lost where I was.\n\nIt’s not that I can’t concentrate at all… it’s more that I get pulled off track pretty easily. Is that helpful?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’ve noticed anything major, no. I mean, sleep can be a little up and down at my age, I suppose, but I wouldn’t say there’s been some big change lately.\n\nI still get to bed and get up… more or less normally. Nothing that jumped out at me as a real problem. Is there a particular kind of change you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your ability to manage everyday tasks and responsibilities has changed over the past year?\n\nPatient: I suppose… yes, a bit. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, because I’m still managing most things day to day.\n\nBut I have had a few mix-ups with plans and responsibilities. Enough that I’ve sort of stepped back from helping lead a couple of the DIY workshops at the center, just so I don’t throw things off. And my wife has been double-checking plans with me more, like for family visits or center activities.\n\nIt’s not like everything has fallen apart, but I guess there has been some change. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any difficulty remembering recent events or learning new information?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in any clear way. I mean, I might forget little things here and there, but I don’t feel like I’m forgetting recent events or unable to learn something new.\n\nIf someone explains something to me and I’m paying attention, I usually get it. So… no, I wouldn’t say that’s been a real problem. Does that sound like what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your mental sharpness has decreased compared to a few years ago?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’d say it’s really decreased, no. Maybe I’m a little slower to get going with things than I used to be, but that could just be age, couldn’t it?\n\nI still feel like I can follow conversations and understand things when I’m focused. So… I wouldn’t want to say I’m less sharp exactly. Does that seem reasonable?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any significant medical conditions or illnesses in the past few years?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. Nothing significant comes to mind, anyway.\n\nI mean, I’ve had the usual bits and pieces you’d expect, but no major illness that I can think of. If there’s something specific you mean, I can try to remember, but I wouldn’t say there’s been anything big.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess it’s mostly that I keep making these little mistakes I feel like I shouldn’t be making. But it has affected a few things. It’s a bit embarrassing to say, but I suppose that counts as interfering some, doesn’t it?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're forgetting things more often than you used to?\n\nPatient: Maybe a little, yes. I mean, sleep can be a little up and down at my age, I suppose, but I wouldn’t say there’s been some big change lately.\n\nI still get to bed and get up… more or less normally. Nothing that jumped out at me as a real problem. Is there a particular kind of change you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your ability to manage everyday tasks and responsibilities has changed over the past year?\n\nPatient: I suppose… yes, a bit. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, because I’m still managing most things day to day.\n\nBut I have had a few mix-ups with plans and responsibilities. Enough that I’ve sort of stepped back from helping lead a couple of the DIY workshops at the center, just so I don’t throw things off. And my wife has been double-checking plans with me more, like for family visits or center activities.\n\nIt’s not like everything has fallen apart, but I guess there has been some change. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any difficulty remembering recent events or learning new information?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in any clear way. I mean, I might forget little things here and there, but I don’t feel like I’m forgetting recent events or unable to learn something new.\n\nIf someone explains something to me and I’m paying attention, I usually get it. So… no, I wouldn’t say that’s been a real problem. Does that sound like what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your mental sharpness has decreased compared to a few years ago?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’d say it’s really decreased, no. Maybe I’m a little slower to get going with things than I used to be, but that could just be age, couldn’t it?\n\nI still feel like I can follow conversations and understand things when I’m focused. So… I wouldn’t want to say I’m less sharp exactly. Does that seem reasonable?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any significant medical conditions or illnesses in the past few years?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. Nothing significant comes to mind, anyway.\n\nI mean, I’ve had the usual bits and pieces you’d expect, but no major illness that I can think of. If there’s something specific you mean, I can try to remember, but I wouldn’t say there’s been anything big.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your coordination or balance lately?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I’ve noticed anything like that. I’m not falling over or feeling unsteady in any new way, at least not that I can think of.\n\nMaybe I’m a bit more careful just because I’m getting older, but I wouldn’t say there’s been a real change in coordination or balance. Is that the sort of thing you mean?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess it’s mostly that I keep making these little mistakes I feel like I shouldn’t be making. It’s embarrassing more than anything, and I thought maybe I should ask about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you remember the correct information at the time, but somehow write or do something different?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s a fair way to put it. It’s not like I don’t know what I meant to write, exactly. Like with the senior-center class sheet, I know the time, but then somehow I’ll put down the wrong one. Or with a board, I’ll measure it, think I’ve got it, and then I miss some little bit of print or detail and it ends up just a hair short. It’s small stuff, but it’s been happening enough that I’m feeling a little foolish about it.\n\nDoctor: Are these mistakes causing you any significant problems or interfering with your daily activities?\n\nPatient: Not major problems, I don’t think… I mean, I can still get through my day. But it has affected a few things. I’ve kind of stepped back from helping lead a couple of the DIY workshops, just because I don’t want my little mix-ups to cause trouble for other people.\n\nAnd at home, my wife has started double-checking plans with me—like family visits or things at the center—so I don’t accidentally get a time or detail wrong and throw things off. It’s a bit embarrassing to say, but I suppose that counts as interfering some, doesn’t it?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're forgetting things more often than you used to?\n\nPatient: Maybe a little, yes. I wouldn’t say I’m forgetting important things all the time or anything like that, but I do spend more time than I’d like looking for things I just had in my hand—my keys, reading glasses, tape measure, that little bag I use for card games.\n\nIt’s usually just a few minutes, and I do find them, so maybe that’s normal to some extent… but it happens enough that I notice it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed these mistakes and forgetfulness getting worse over time?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure, to be honest. I don’t know that I could say it’s clearly getting worse over time. It’s more that I’ve started noticing it more, maybe because it’s embarrassing when it happens.\n\nMy wife might have a better sense of whether it’s increasing, but from my side… I’d say it comes and goes a bit. Nothing dramatic, I don’t think. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or worried about these mistakes or forgetfulness?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say anxious exactly… maybe a little self-conscious about it, if that makes sense. I mean, sleep can be a little up and down at my age, I suppose, but I wouldn’t say there’s been some big change lately.\n\nI still get to bed and get up… more or less normally. Nothing that jumped out at me as a real problem. Is there a particular kind of change you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your ability to manage everyday tasks and responsibilities has changed over the past year?\n\nPatient: I suppose… yes, a bit. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, because I’m still managing most things day to day.\n\nBut I have had a few mix-ups with plans and responsibilities. Enough that I’ve sort of stepped back from helping lead a couple of the DIY workshops at the center, just so I don’t throw things off. And my wife has been double-checking plans with me more, like for family visits or center activities.\n\nIt’s not like everything has fallen apart, but I guess there has been some change. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any difficulty remembering recent events or learning new information?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in any clear way. I mean, I might forget little things here and there, but I don’t feel like I’m forgetting recent events or unable to learn something new.\n\nIf someone explains something to me and I’m paying attention, I usually get it. So… no, I wouldn’t say that’s been a real problem. Does that sound like what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your mental sharpness has decreased compared to a few years ago?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’d say it’s really decreased, no. Maybe I’m a little slower to get going with things than I used to be, but that could just be age, couldn’t it?\n\nI still feel like I can follow conversations and understand things when I’m focused. So… I wouldn’t want to say I’m less sharp exactly. Does that seem reasonable?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any significant medical conditions or illnesses in the past few years?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. Nothing significant comes to mind, anyway.\n\nI mean, I’ve had the usual bits and pieces you’d expect, but no major illness that I can think of. If there’s something specific you mean, I can try to remember, but I wouldn’t say there’s been anything big.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your coordination or balance lately?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I’ve noticed anything like that. I’m not falling over or feeling unsteady in any new way, at least not that I can think of.\n\nMaybe I’m a bit more careful just because I’m getting older, but I wouldn’t say there’s been a real change in coordination or balance. Is that the sort of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your memory or thinking abilities have gotten worse over the past few years?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in any major way. I mean, I forget the odd thing here and there, but I’ve always been a bit like that, so I’m not sure I’d call it getting worse.\n\nMy thinking feels about the same to me, I suppose. If I’m concentrating, I can manage things well enough. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. It’s embarrassing more than anything, and I thought maybe I should ask about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you remember the correct information at the time, but somehow write or do something different?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s a fair way to put it. It’s not like I don’t know what I meant to write, exactly. Like with the senior-center class sheet, I know the time, but then somehow I’ll put down the wrong one. It’s small stuff, but it’s been happening enough that I’m feeling a little foolish about it.\n\nDoctor: Are these mistakes causing you any significant problems or interfering with your daily activities?\n\nPatient: Not major problems, I don’t think… I mean, I can still get through my day. But it has affected a few things. I’ve kind of stepped back from helping lead a couple of the DIY workshops, just because I don’t want my little mix-ups to cause trouble for other people.\n\nAnd at home, my wife has started double-checking plans with me—like family visits or things at the center—so I don’t accidentally get a time or detail wrong and throw things off. It’s a bit embarrassing to say, but I suppose that counts as interfering some, doesn’t it?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're forgetting things more often than you used to?\n\nPatient: Maybe a little, yes. I wouldn’t say I’m forgetting important things all the time or anything like that, but I do spend more time than I’d like looking for things I just had in my hand—my keys, reading glasses, tape measure, that little bag I use for card games.\n\nIt’s usually just a few minutes, and I do find them, so maybe that’s normal to some extent… but it happens enough that I notice it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed these mistakes and forgetfulness getting worse over time?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure, to be honest. I don’t know that I could say it’s clearly getting worse over time. It’s more that I’ve started noticing it more, maybe because it’s embarrassing when it happens.\n\nMy wife might have a better sense of whether it’s increasing, but from my side… I’d say it comes and goes a bit. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or worried about these mistakes or forgetfulness?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say anxious exactly… maybe a little self-conscious about it, if that makes sense. At least, not enough that I’d have thought to mention it. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your ability to concentrate or pay attention?\n\nPatient: A little, yes. Not terrible, I don’t think, but I do notice it sometimes.\n\nLike at the senior center, if I’m filling something out or working on one of those little projects, and people are talking at the next table, or my phone lights up, my mind just sort of jumps over there. Then I look back and I’ve lost where I was.\n\nIt’s not that I can’t concentrate at all… it’s more that I get pulled off track pretty easily. Is that helpful?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’ve noticed anything major, no. I mean, sleep can be a little up and down at my age, I suppose, but I wouldn’t say there’s been some big change lately.\n\nI still get to bed and get up… more or less normally. Nothing that jumped out at me as a real problem. Is there a particular kind of change you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your ability to manage everyday tasks and responsibilities has changed over the past year?\n\nPatient: I suppose… yes, a bit. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, because I’m still managing most things day to day.\n\nBut I have had a few mix-ups with plans and responsibilities. Enough that I’ve sort of stepped back from helping lead a couple of the DIY workshops at the center, just so I don’t throw things off. And my wife has been double-checking plans with me more, like for family visits or center activities.\n\nIt’s not like everything has fallen apart, but I guess there has been some change. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any difficulty remembering recent events or learning new information?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in any clear way. I mean, I might forget little things here and there, but I don’t feel like I’m forgetting recent events or unable to learn something new.\n\nIf someone explains something to me and I’m paying attention, I usually get it. So… no, I wouldn’t say that’s been a real problem. Does that sound like what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your mental sharpness has decreased compared to a few years ago?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’d say it’s really decreased, no. Maybe I’m a little slower to get going with things than I used to be, but that could just be age, couldn’t it?\n\nI still feel like I can follow conversations and understand things when I’m focused. So… I wouldn’t want to say I’m less sharp exactly. Does that seem reasonable?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any significant medical conditions or illnesses in the past few years?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. Nothing significant comes to mind, anyway.\n\nI mean, I’ve had the usual bits and pieces you’d expect, but no major illness that I can think of. If there’s something specific you mean, I can try to remember, but I wouldn’t say there’s been anything big.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your coordination or balance lately?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I’ve noticed anything like that. I’m not falling over or feeling unsteady in any new way, at least not that I can think of.\n\nMaybe I’m a bit more careful just because I’m getting older, but I wouldn’t say there’s been a real change in coordination or balance. If I’m concentrating, I can manage things well enough. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your overall ability to think and remember has declined significantly compared to a few years ago?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn’t say significantly. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or worried about these mistakes or forgetfulness?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say anxious exactly… maybe a little self-conscious about it, if that makes sense. I don’t sit around worrying all day, but when I realize I’ve mixed something up, I do feel embarrassed and I start hoping nobody thinks I’m being careless.\n\nSo… yes, maybe some worry, but not in a big way. I’m not sure if that’s the kind of thing you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I have my moments like anyone, I suppose, especially when I feel a bit foolish about forgetting something, but I wouldn’t say I’ve been sad or down lately.\n\nI think overall my mood’s been okay. Does that sound like what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your ability to learn new things or follow instructions?\n\nPatient: Maybe a little, though I wouldn’t want to overstate it. It’s not that I can’t learn things, exactly. It’s more like… if someone tells me something out loud, I don’t always take it in the first time.\n\nLike my wife will be looking right at me and say, “Dave, the cards are in the hall closet now,” and then a minute later I realize I heard her talking, but I didn’t actually catch where she said they were. It’s a bit embarrassing. Is that the sort of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself struggling to find the right words when you're trying to express something?\n\nPatient: Not that I’ve really noticed, no. I mean, once in a while I might lose a word, but I think that happens to everybody, doesn’t it?\n\nI don’t feel like I’m regularly stuck trying to say what I mean. At least, not enough that I’d have thought to mention it. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your ability to concentrate or pay attention?\n\nPatient: A little, yes. Not terrible, I don’t think, but I do notice it sometimes.\n\nLike at the senior center, if I’m filling something out or working on one of those little projects, and people are talking at the next table, or my phone lights up, my mind just sort of jumps over there. Then I look back and I’ve lost where I was.\n\nIt’s not that I can’t concentrate at all… it’s more that I get pulled off track pretty easily. Is that helpful?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’ve noticed anything major, no. I mean, sleep can be a little up and down at my age, I suppose, but I wouldn’t say there’s been some big change lately.\n\nI still get to bed and get up… more or less normally. Nothing that jumped out at me as a real problem. Is there a particular kind of change you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your ability to manage everyday tasks and responsibilities has changed over the past year?\n\nPatient: I suppose… yes, a bit. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, because I’m still managing most things day to day.\n\nBut I have had a few mix-ups with plans and responsibilities. Enough that I’ve sort of stepped back from helping lead a couple of the DIY workshops at the center, just so I don’t throw things off. And my wife has been double-checking plans with me more, like for family visits or center activities.\n\nIt’s not like everything has fallen apart, but I guess there has been some change. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any difficulty remembering recent events or learning new information?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in any clear way. I mean, I might forget little things here and there, but I don’t feel like I’m forgetting recent events or unable to learn something new.\n\nIf someone explains something to me and I’m paying attention, I usually get it. So… no, I wouldn’t say that’s been a real problem. Does that sound like what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your mental sharpness has decreased compared to a few years ago?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’d say it’s really decreased, no. Maybe I’m a little slower to get going with things than I used to be, but that could just be age, couldn’t it?\n\nI still feel like I can follow conversations and understand things when I’m focused. So… I wouldn’t want to say I’m less sharp exactly. Does that seem reasonable?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any significant medical conditions or illnesses in the past few years?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so. Nothing significant comes to mind, anyway.\n\nI mean, I’ve had the usual bits and pieces you’d expect, but no major illness that I can think of. If there’s something specific you mean, I can try to remember, but I wouldn’t say there’s been anything big.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your coordination or balance lately?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I’ve noticed anything like that. I’m not falling over or feeling unsteady in any new way, at least not that I can think of.\n\nMaybe I’m a bit more careful just because I’m getting older, but I wouldn’t say there’s been a real change in coordination or balance. Is that the sort of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your memory or thinking abilities have gotten worse over the past few years?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not in any major way. I mean, I forget the odd thing here and there, but I’ve always been a bit like that, so I’m not sure I’d call it getting worse.\n\nMy thinking feels about the same to me, I suppose. If I’m concentrating, I can manage things well enough. Does that answer what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your overall ability to think and remember has declined significantly compared to a few years ago?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn’t say significantly. I mean, I have the normal forgetful moments, but compared with a few years ago, I don’t feel like there’s been a big decline.\n\nAt least, not that I’ve noticed. If there’s something specific you’re looking for, I can try to think it through, but overall I’d say I’m about the same.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your mental abilities have been declining gradually over time?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. Not gradually declining, at least not in a way that stands out to me.\n\nI suppose I have the usual moments where I misplace something or need a second to think of a word, but I wouldn’t want to make it sound worse than it is. Overall, I feel like I’m managing about the same. Is that what you mean?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.   },
